- **Why:** The owner, 21 November: "please also check for overlapping textures on two surfaces that are flush on the same grid/coordinates". The first run found about 300 in one showroom and 900 in the town; most came from shared code (the canal fence's rails at corners, the canal trims where two walls meet, bar rows' crossbars, the town's sills, lintels, door frames and pilasters), fixed there.
- **How:** Leave a 1 unit step between pieces that meet; map_build.fence, bar_row and map_ring's trims do it now.
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py flush (axis-aligned faces; a face buried in another brush or looking into the void outside the map, by the build's own flood fill, is not counted)
- **Tags:** geometry, owner, check

### Copy City 18 itself (`copy-city18`)

Where a piece needs architecture, take it from the owner's map: their materials (palette.json), their ways (the reference pictures), and their very brushes, lifted as prefabs (map_prefab.py: a box of their map with every face's texture exactly as laid, placed again moved and turned) for facade bays, shopfronts, window frames, door surrounds, balconies, cornices, stairs. Some of their streets get their facades from painted building textures (building_template/*, building_trainstation_*), not brushes: those textures are theirs to use too.

- **Why:** The owner, 21 November: "don't be afraid to more or less rip off and copy the design principles, textures, etc from city 18; the modules are really bad with texture choice and architecture".
- **How:** python3 map_prefab.py extract c18_lc.vmf NAME X1 Y1 Z1 X2 Y2 Z2 --anchor X Y Z --note ...; in a module: from map_prefab import place; place(m, NAME, u, v, z, turn). The library: _dev/reference/prefabs/ (python3 map_prefab.py list).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** facades, materials, city18, owner

### Tall walls in bands (`valve-wall-bands`)

A wall taller than about 256 is never one texture. Lay it in courses: a base (stonewall050c, floor to 96), the body (050a), a band course (050b, about 192 to 320), the body again, a top course (050e). Each course is its own brush, the base and the band 4 to 8 proud of the body, so the wall casts a line of shadow at each change.

- **Why:** Valve's d1_trainstation_01 does it on every big wall (counted 21 November from the owner's upload): one material over 400 units of wall reads as a placeholder, the courses give it scale and age. In every old-city Valve map measured the first change of material is most often 128 over the wall's foot (d1_trainstation_02: 870 wall columns, 04: 1343, 03: 290, d3_c17_13: 781, Ravenholm: 426), then 64; 64 to 83% of wall length over 256 tall is banded, against 15% in the Citadel.
- **How:** Stack boxes by height with the materials above (Mod.box per course), or lift a banded piece of wall with map_prefab.py. City 18's own stone (stonewall050f) fits the same scheme. Route Kanal's deep canals (d1_canals_08, 12, 13): walls lean back about 14 degrees off upright, step 36 at about 352 up and carry a coping at the top, 476 to 508 over the floor (Route Kanal picture R4).
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py bare: a face of one material 384 long and 256 tall with nothing within 24 in front of it (printed by every build as LOOK: bare).
- **Tags:** facades, materials, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/01_kleiner_lab_cut.png`, `valve_c17/12_shop_street_eye.png`, `valve_canals/04_battered_canal_section.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/02_overview_ne.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/12_courtyard_plan.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/03_station_front.png`

### Columns on a rhythm (`valve-column-rhythm`)

Big halls and long walls are broken into bays by columns, pilasters or posts at one steady spacing (Valve: 368 along the platforms, 198 in narrower halls), with beams or trusses over them; benches, bins and fences are set out on the same rhythm.

- **Why:** Valve's station (pictures V5, V6, V10, V11): a long blank wall or open hall gives the eye nothing to measure; bays make the space read and give cover and corners.
- **How:** In a hall module, place a column every 320 to 384 (a multiple of 64 that divides the length), a beam between their tops, and dress each bay the same way with small variations.
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py bare: a face of one material 384 long and 256 tall with nothing within 24 in front of it (printed by every build as LOOK: bare).
- **Tags:** geometry, interiors, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/06_square_colonnade_cut.png`, `valve_c17/13_road_tunnel_eye.png`, `valve_c17/14_road_tunnel_section.png`, `valve_c17/24_nexus_park_eye.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/05_payphone.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/06_trash.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/08_platform.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/10_hall_plan.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/11_platform_plan.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/03_station_front.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/06_station_hall_cut.png`

### Light in pools, not floods (`valve-light-pools`)

Let places be dim and light them in pools: a fixture over each door, each way on and each thing to look at, with darker stretches between. Valve lights the whole train station with 23 light, 40 light_spot and 19 point_spotlight.

- **Why:** Pictures V3 and V8: the lit doorway at the end of a dark passage leads the player there without a word (house rule 10, let the world show it). Evenly lit rooms look flat. Ravenholm: 26 lights in the whole town, its 11 spotlights about 500 apart; the flats: plain lights at 8 as fill and light_spot at 400.
- **How:** One fixture per bay or door, about a third left off (as the canal walls do), light_spot under lamps that hang; brighter where people gather. Route Kanal: cage lamps on the canal walls about 811 apart (581 to 956), 311 up, each with its own light (brightness about 70); light_spot brightness 1200 (500 to 1850); 346 of 523 lights warm.
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py offlamp checks that an off lamp has no light; pools are judged in the viewer.
- **Tags:** lighting, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/13_road_tunnel_eye.png`, `valve_c17/20_warehouse_hall_eye.png`, `valve_canals/10_city_canal_eye.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/03_platform.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/07_interrogation.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/08_platform.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_03/04_corridor.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_05/04_teleport.png`, `valve_d3_citadel_01/04_pipe.png`

### Clutter on the edges (`valve-clutter`)

Dress floors with small loose things (bottles, cans, papers, boxes, suitcases, crates, push carts) along walls, at column feet and in corners, never in the walking line, plus decals and overlays (Valve: 178 physics props, 116 decals and 29 overlays in one station).

- **Why:** Picture V3: the crates and the cabinet hug one wall and leave the way clear; clutter in the walking line only blocks people, clutter on the edges makes a place lived in.
- **How:** Mod.grime for decals; small physics props through m.dynamic (never prop_static, vbsp deletes them: physics-props-dynamic). Keep a clear lane 96 wide through every room and door. Route Kanal: within 224 of the canal's middle about 4 fixed and 4 loose props per 1024 of city canal, about 1 in the wide canals (the boat's lane clear); commonest oildrum001 264 (and 141 explosive), shrub_01a 190, driftwood 238, ivy_01 110, wood crates 184, rebar 97, cargo containers 93, concrete debris piles 74. Ours scatters about 2.4 per 1024 in a plain stretch. Measured wall gaps (21 November): 45% of statics and 16% of physics props touch a wall, another 4% and 22% within 16; 31% of statics and 25% of physics stand more than 128 from any wall (yard and canal clutter in groups).
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py overlap and map_floating.py catch clutter inside or above things; the clear lane is judged in the viewer.
- **Tags:** props, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/07_tree_street_eye.png`, `valve_canals/08_city_canal_iso.png`, `valve_canals/16_shanty_camp_iso.png`, `valve_canals/19_gun_station_iso.png`, `valve_canals/20_boxcar_hideout_iso.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/03_platform.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/05_payphone.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_01/11_platform_plan.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/04_plaza.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_04/03_alley.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_05/03_lab.png`, `valve_d3_c17_13/02_overview_ne.png`, `valve_d3_c17_13/04_square.png`, `valve_d3_c17_13/05_barricade.png`

### Streets in Valve's proportions (`valve-street-proportions`)

A City 17 street is about 640 to 768 wall to wall: a roadway of 256 (320 on the main war street), pavements of 128 to 192 each side, fronts 576 to 672 tall over it (height about half the width). Side streets and war-torn ones are lower (walls 256 to 384).

- **Why:** Cross-sections through outdoor AI nodes (21 November): d1_trainstation_04, 36 with a kerb each side: road 256 (30 of 36), pavements 192, 128, 256, wall to wall 640, walls median 576 (height over width 0.56); d1_trainstation_03: road 256, walls 672; d3_c17_13, 83 sections: road 320 (64), pavements 128 (45) and 192, wall to wall 640, walls median 256 (0.31); d1_trainstation_02 plaza roads 416 to 608, walls 672 (0.41). Pictures T3.3, T4.4, W3.
- **How:** Size street modules on these where the owner has not settled a number; where it differs from the owner's own streets (street-profile: pavements 96), the owner's wins: ask before changing.
- **Checked by:** review (the viewer from a pavement eye)
- **Tags:** streets, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/03_checkpoint_street_section.png`, `valve_c17/10_shop_street_section.png`, `valve_c17/24_nexus_park_eye.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_03/03_street.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_04/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_04/04_street.png`, `valve_d3_c17_13/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d3_c17_13/03_street.png`

### Upper storeys and far blocks as painted fronts (`valve-template-fronts`)

Where the player cannot reach, Valve's fronts are flat walls in building_template textures (windows, sills and cornices painted on), with window models (building_tem002_window01 and its _bars) set into them near the player; built detail (plinth, shopfronts, doors, real windows) only at street level.

- **Why:** building_template share of all vertical wall area: 46% in d1_trainstation_02, 39% in 04, 38% in d3_c17_13, 35% in 03 (3% in the lab, 0 in Ravenholm, the coast and the Citadel); d1_trainstation_03 has building_tem002_window01 14, the same with bars 14, courtyard_template002_window 10. Pictures T2.1, T2.7, T4.6.
- **How:** Backdrop blocks and storeys out of reach: one template face per storey band instead of brush windows; keep map_town.facade where players walk. Check the materials exist in the owner's content first (map_assets.py). Past the template fronts, the 3D skybox (scale 16 in every map) holds only 1 to 47 models, nearly all skybox buildings (row_res_2, row_upscale, project_building01_skybox, buildingskybox_002a, the Citadel, tree cards) and a few ground brushes; brushes more than 1024 from play are 13% of a city map, plain six-faced boxes with 29% of their faces nodraw.
- **Checked by:** map_assets.py (the materials exist); the look in the viewer
- **Tags:** facades, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/08_back_court_blocks_iso.png`, `valve_c17/12_shop_street_eye.png`, `valve_c17/32_nexus_outside_iso.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/05_courtyard.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/07_apartment_block.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_03/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_04/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_04/06_block.png`

### Flats at Valve's size (`valve-flat-interiors`)

A City 17 flat: ceiling 120 over the floor (112 to 128), corridors 64 to 128 wide, rooms about 160 to 232 by 224 to 376, doorways 48 wide and 108 high (door01_left; 96 for a pair), stairs 8 by 12 in their own shaft; furniture only against the walls (radiators under windows, shelves, a couch, a TV), a bulb or dome light per room and every few doors in a corridor.

- **Why:** d1_trainstation_03: floor to ceiling 120 at 81 of 125 indoor AI nodes (112: 17, 128: 11); rooms median 160 by 324; doorways 48x108 and 60x108; radiator01a 16 (the commonest model), shelves 23, couches 7, lightbulb01a 12, light_domelight02_on 8. Ceilings are 120 in Ravenholm too (19 of 41) and d3_c17_13 (87 of 157), 128 in d1_trainstation_04 (32 of 75); halls and labs 248 to 352. Pictures T3.4 to T3.7, R7. The uprising agrees (21 November): ceilings 120 at 294 nodes in d3_c17_05, 263 in 06b, 124 in 07; doorways 48x108 (24), 96x108 (10), 56 and 59 by 108 (6); Valve's standards room explains the 120: a storey of 128 with an 8 floor (valve-standard-sizes).
- **How:** Interior modules: 120 to 128 in flats and houses, taller only for halls, shops and Combine places; doorway 48 by 108 for door01_left. Small rooms work because they are sparse (room-to-move).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** interiors, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/09_court_flats_cut.png`, `valve_c17/15_raided_flats_cut.png`, `valve_c17/16_flat_room_eye.png`, `valve_c17/17_combine_held_flats_cut.png`, `valve_c17/19_flats_corridor_eye.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/07_houses_cut.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_03/04_corridor.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_03/05_flat.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_03/06_stairwell.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_03/07_block_cut.png`

### Boards nailed across, at Valve's size (`valve-boarded-openings`)

Boarded doors and windows are plank brushes 4 thick, 8 wide and 112 long (or 16 wide and 64 to 112 long), a few across the opening at slightly different angles, in a door or floor wood (wooddoor045b, wooddoor048a, wooddoor015a, woodfloor005a).

- **Why:** Plank brushes counted (21 November): 172 in d1_trainstation_03 (78 of them 4 by 8 by 112), 39 in Ravenholm (4x8x112, 4x16x64), 30 in d3_c17_13 (4x16x112, wooddoor015a), 35 in d1_trainstation_04. Pictures R3, T4.5.
- **How:** A board helper: 3 to 5 planks of 4 by 8 by 112 across a 48 by 108 doorway or a window, func_detail, each turned a few degrees; for the infestation quarter's houses and the town's boarded doorways. Three more Ravenholm maps agree: plank brushes 119 in d1_town_01, 71 in 02, 49 in 01a (woodfloor005a most, then wooddoor004a, 015a, 032a; func_detail 176 of 239), and two in three are turned off the axes (78 of 119 in 01); 22 more boards are physics props held still, 14 of them freed when grabbed, 73% touching a wall. Ravenholm: a zombie behind boards breaks through: d1_town_01 bldg1's four board props Spawn the zombie behind them from OnHealthChanged (template flags 2690, wait for script); its sequence (Idle01, then attackE) Breaks the boards, and its OnDeath Breaks all four; d1_town_02 bldg1_zombie3 the same with brush boards Killed.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** interiors, facades, valve, infestation, ravenholm

- **See:** `valve_c17/15_raided_flats_cut.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/03_street.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_04/05_attic.png`

### Wires over the streets (`valve-overhead-cables`)

Streets carry cables: spans of 700 to 900 between poles and wall mounts, slack 100, width 1 to 2; poles 700 to 1400 apart, or utilitypolemount01a on the walls.

- **Why:** Rope spans median 734 (d1_trainstation_02, 25 spans), 736 (04, 46), 914 (Ravenholm, 32 spans from 41 rope entities), slack 100 in all; utilitypole02b 693 and 1411 apart, utilitypole01b 887 in Ravenholm; utilitypolemount01a 8 in d1_trainstation_04. Pictures R3, T2.4, T4.4.
- **How:** keyframe_rope and move_rope from pole to pole and pole to wall mount, never ending in the air (no-floating-ropes). Rope settings in all 22 maps (781 rope entities): RopeMaterial cable/cable 98% (cable/rope 17), Subdiv 2 (67%) or 5; spans under 128 slack 50 and width 1 (wires between substation boxes, connectors, swings, pulleys), 128 to 1024 slack 100 and width 1 to 2, over 1024 (power towers) slack 120 and width 3; ends pole to pole 276, box to box 24, box to pole 18. Our m.rope defaults to slack 40 and our scratch town had slack 0 to 2.
- **Checked by:** no-floating-ropes check; review
- **Tags:** streets, props, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/04_street_wires_eye.png`, `valve_c17/36_rubble_wired_street_eye.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/03_street.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/04_plaza.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_04/04_street.png`

### The Combine is one cold metal; the old city is banded and warm (`valve-combine-cold`)

Combine places: one metal over whole walls, no courses, lit cold and bright (light_spot and point_spotlight '144 224 240' to '159 237 215'), clean (no decals), teal or blue fog where there is fog. The old city round them: banded walls, warm light ('255 224 144'), grime.

- **Why:** d3_citadel_01: citadel_metalwall077a on 6816 faces, only 15% of tall wall length changes material (the old-city maps: 64 to 83%), 28 light_spot at a median 2000, 17 point_spotlight '147 226 240', fog '67 107 107', 0 decals and 0 overlays; d2_coast_12 bunkers: 27 point_spotlight, light_spot median 2500, '144 224 240'; the flats of d1_trainstation_03: 25 of 37 lights '255 224 144'. Pictures C1, C3, C5, K3.
- **How:** Combine modules: one Combine metal per wall, cold spots, no grime decals (combine-kit for the props); old-city modules: valve-wall-bands, warm lights, grime.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** combine, lighting, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/26_nexus_core_cut.png`, `valve_c17/27_nexus_combine_corridor_eye.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_02/02_overview_ne.png`, `valve_d1_trainstation_04/02_overview_ne.png`, `valve_d2_coast_12/02_overview_ne.png`, `valve_d2_coast_12/03_bunker_mouth.png`, `valve_d3_c17_13/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d3_c17_13/03_street.png`, `valve_d3_citadel_01/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d3_citadel_01/02_overview_ne.png`, `valve_d3_citadel_01/03_cliff_cut.png`, `valve_d3_citadel_01/05_combine_inside.png`, `valve_d3_citadel_01/06_rock_and_metal.png`

### Ravenholm's dark: weak cold light and warm pools (`raven-dusk-light`)

Valve darkens Ravenholm without fog: a weak cold sun and almost no ambient under a dusk sky, then pools of warm light from spotlights on the houses (a point_spotlight with a light_spot at the same spot, '255 245 145', 400 to 1000) about 500 apart, dim fills of 8 under them, and the orange glow of a fire.

- **Why:** d1_town_03: light_environment '175 230 239' at 50, ambient '43 45 57' at 5 (the City 17 maps: '237 218 143' at 800, ambient 100); sky_day01_09; no env_fog_controller; 11 point_spotlight each paired with a light_spot, nearest 506 apart; 8 light at 8; an env_fire with '254 174 10' at 300; 22 of its 26 lights warm. Pictures R3, R4.
- **How:** One map has one sun, so map_raven.py's sheet of toolsblocklight under the quarter's sky is the right stand-in for Valve's weak sun; what Valve adds is the pools: a light_spotlight01_lamp on a house wall every 500 or so with a point_spotlight and a light_spot, a fire in a drum, against the gloom lights (now four at 7, cold grey). Ask the owner before changing the quarter's light. d1_town_01, 01a, 02 and 02a share the sun '175 230 239 50' and ambient '43 45 57 5' under sky_day01_09; only the graveyard (02a) adds fog, black '0 0 0' from 1500 to 4000; the mine (04) has no sun; the way out (05) brightens to '254 242 160 400' with warm fog 1024 to 10000. Spotlight spacing by map: 294 (01), 368 (02), 452 (01a), 540 (02a), 780 (04).
- **Checked by:** review (a screenshot from the owner: the quarter's light is a first guess)
- **Tags:** lighting, infestation, ravenholm, valve

### Ravenholm's lanes: house to house, timber and plaster (`raven-streets-and-houses`)

Ravenholm's lanes have no kerb and no pavement: cobbles and uneven ground run house to house, 384 to 768 wall to wall; houses two or three storeys (walls 256 to 512 over the lane), timber and plaster on a concrete plinth (to 128, 48 or 16), roofs pitched 20 to 35 degrees in corrugated metal or tile, a steep mansard or spire for a landmark; boards on openings, wires overhead.

- **Why:** d1_town_03: no kerb in 119 cross-sections; 36% of the floor is displacement; wall to wall most often 640; walls median 352 (height over width 0.42); woodfloor005a the commonest material (961 faces), plasterwall029a/c/d/g, brickwall048a/d/i, concretewall032d under plaster or metal (first change at 128, 48, 16); roof pitch 25 degrees (31 faces), 20, 35, steep faces 65 to 70; metalwall026a and 014a, tileroof003a and 004b. Pictures R1 to R3.
- **How:** The quarter's main street keeps the owner's street recipe (they asked for it); use these numbers for the lanes, yards and back ways behind it and for the houses' looks. The other maps agree: kerbs in 4 of 320 cross-sections in d1_town_01 and 0 of 50 in 01a (the town square in 02 has kerbs 4 to 15 in 21 of 275); wall to wall median 620 (01), 376 (01a, walls 384 to 512: height over width 0.9), 589 (02); the lane floor is dirt and cobbles (01: blenddirtgrass008a 36%, stonefloor006a/b/c 31%, planks 8%). Ours: the quarter's main street keeps the owner's crown, kerb 8 and pavements 96; its back lanes and yards should have none.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** streets, facades, infestation, ravenholm, valve

- **See:** `valve_d1_town_03/01_overview_sw.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/02_overview_ne.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/03_street.png`

### A way over the roofs (`raven-rooftop-refuge`)

Give an overrun quarter a second way through above the dead: low roofs joined by plank bridges and ladders, timber scaffolds, one high place to hold (Grigori's roof), reached from inside a house.

- **Why:** d1_town_03: 74% of the AI nodes are outdoors, many on roofs; metalladder002 4 and 002b 2, plank bridges of woodfloor005a; npc_monk on a roof at z -3096, about 500 over the streets; explosive barrels set on roofs for the roof zombies. Pictures R4, R6.
- **How:** In map_raven.py: two or three houses with a stair to a flat roof, planks to the next roof, a ladder down at the far end.
- **Checked by:** review; valid-viewpoints for spots on the roofs
- **Tags:** layout, infestation, ravenholm, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/30_nexus_roof_post_iso.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/02_overview_ne.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/04_rooftops.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/06_corpse_alley.png`

### Traps lying ready, the dead from makers (`raven-traps-and-makers`)

Leave traps as loose physics props where the dead come: propane tanks in clusters of 2 to 6, explosive barrels in twos and threes, saw blades to throw, one rigged machine trap at a choke point; bring the dead with npc_maker and npc_template_maker and walk them in with aiscripted_schedule; health kits as rewards.

- **Why:** d1_town_03: 13 propane_tank001a, 10 oildrum001_explosive, 4 sawblade001a, a propeller trap (engine, blade, lever, func_rotating); 15 npc_zombie and 2 npc_fastzombie, 10 npc_maker, 7 npc_template_maker, 14 aiscripted_schedule; 18 item_healthkit. The same machinery map_raven.py uses. Pictures R5, R7.
- **How:** map_raven.py: clusters of tanks and barrels along the makers' walk and one trap at a choke; keep them physics (physics-props-dynamic). Across d1_town_01 to 05: 28 of 31 propane tanks lie on their side, 5 on a table, 58% within 16 of a wall, about 36 apart; explosive barrels stand (65 of 67), spread (nearest other explosive median 128, q25 57) among plain oildrum001 (91), skins not 0 on 28%; graveyard canisters stand in twos and fours 33 apart. Ours: three single blades, three explosive drums and a pallet, all upright, spawnflags 0. The rest of Ravenholm (d1_town_01, 04): the spinning blade is a TrapPropeller_Blade prop_physics (Damagetype 1) on a phys_motor (speed -1200, inertiafactor 5) switched by a toggle func_button (flags 1057, wait 5) with spin-up, loop and spin-down sounds and a sputter on a random 3 to 8 s timer, the first already running at map start (logic_auto Press); the mine's bladed cart (func_physbox, massScale 4) is pushed down the rails by a phys_thruster (force 1000) with a trigger_hurt (damage 250, flags 3) parented to its func_rotating (maxspeed 1500), while four trigger_onces 640 to 770 apart along the rails each spawn 2 to 4 zombies that run at the player (the cart's run is the blade's line); 142 of the 154 makers hold one NPC, fired once by Spawn (raven-gas-fire-trap, raven-car-drop-trap, raven-live-fence).
- **Checked by:** map_lint (physics props); review
- **Tags:** props, infestation, ravenholm, valve

- **See:** `valve_d1_town_03/05_trap_passage.png`, `valve_d1_town_03/07_houses_cut.png`

### Abandoned shows as litter, ivy and leaves; one corpse tells the rest (`raven-litter-not-gore`)

Dress an overrun quarter with trash, ivy and dead-leaf decals and a few scorch marks rather than blood (Valve's Ravenholm has none); a corpse is a single set piece (a pinned ragdoll with crows round it), not scattered.

- **Why:** d1_town_03: 82 infodecal, 0 of them blood: trashdecal01a 26, ivy03 19, leaves_ground02 17, plaster020a 5, graffiti; 2 info_overlay, both scorch1; 1 prop_ragdoll (corpse1) with 5 npc_crow. Picture R6.
- **How:** Mod.grime with trash, ivy and leaves in the quarter; at most one corpse scene a yard (the zombies bring their own blood in game). Only d1_town_03 is without blood: the rest of Ravenholm has 189 blood decals (146 in d1_town_01), half of them within 128 of a body; keep the lanes to litter, ivy and leaves and put blood only at a body scene (raven-dead-set-pieces).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** grime, infestation, ravenholm, valve

- **See:** `valve_d1_town_03/06_corpse_alley.png`

### The quarter's sound: four soundscapes and placed scares (`raven-sound`)

Name d1_town.Street outside, d1_town.WoodBuilding or d1_town.ConcreteBuilding inside by what the house is made of, d1_town.CorpseRoom where the dead wait; put one-shot sounds (a metal grate, a far fast-zombie call, an attack, a fire loop) where something should startle.

- **Why:** d1_town_03: 7 env_soundscape (ConcreteBuilding 3, Street 2, WoodBuilding 1, CorpseRoom 1), radius 128; 27 ambient_generic: zo_attack2 4, metalgrate3 4, fz_alert_far1 3, tram_hit1 2, smallfireloop 2.
- **How:** m.soundscape with these names in map_raven.py (map_modules SCAPES already has d1_town.WoodBuilding as 'house'). The other six Ravenholm maps: 101 env_soundscape (d1_town.WoodBuilding 23, Street 22, Start 15, Rooftop 14, ConcreteBuilding 11, CorpseRoom 5; in the mine WaterCave 2, CaveTunnel 2, MineshaftDown 1, HeadcrabCave 1), all radius 128; 183 ambient_generic, 142 one-shot (flags 48), radius median 1250; d1_town.Flies from trigger_multiples 13 to 45 from six corpses; board creaks from func_physbox OnAwakened (d1_town_04); distant poison zombie calls (radius 3000) @0, 0.7, 1.2 and 2 from 556 to 1316 away as the graveyard opens.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** sound, infestation, ravenholm, valve

### A canal floor falls to its middle (`valve-canal-dish`)

Let the canal's floor fall gently from the walls to the middle line: Valve's city canal dishes about 88 over its 512 (the floor meets the walls 44 up, median), its wide canals 36 to 48 over 1024 (8 to 10 degrees). Keep the owner's 45 degree banks at the walls (they asked for the sloped sides) and the dry mud; sag the mud between the banks' feet 16 to 32 toward the middle line, so water would run down the middle.

- **Why:** Measured across 372 open cuts of the 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November): the floor meets the walls 44 up (city canal) and 90 up (wide canals) at the median, few floors are flat; true 45 degree banks only in d1_canals_10, and there 284 high. Route Kanal pictures (_dev/reference/valve_canals/) R1, R3, R5, R17.
- **How:** The canal's mud displacement (Channel.dress / dress_canal_walls): lower its middle rows 16 to 32 under F, keeping the edges at F where they meet the banks and at every module's ends (u 0 and L stay the plain section).
- **Checked by:** review (a cut across in the viewer: map_view.py --eye on the floor looking along, or a slice)
- **Tags:** canal, valve, geometry

- **See:** `valve_canals/01_city_canal_section.png`, `valve_canals/03_airboat_canal_section.png`, `valve_canals/05_banked_canal_section.png`, `valve_canals/17_slime_iso.png`

### Long canal stretches get a walk along one wall (`valve-canal-ledges`)

Most of Valve's canal has a ledge a person can walk along on at least one side: in the city canal 72 wide (42 to 106) at 212 up (146 to 256), in the wide canals 102 wide (64 to 148) at 316 up, railed with handrail04 props (40 high) where it is high. Give some modules (not every one) a walk on one wall, 64 to 128 wide, 128 to 256 over the floor, reached by steps or a ladder, ending on the plain section at u 0 and L.

- **Why:** 28 of 36 city canal cuts and 200 of 318 wide canal cuts have a walkable ledge (32 wide or more, 72 clear over it). Route Kanal pictures (_dev/reference/valve_canals/) R2, R7, R8, R18.
- **How:** m.det for the slab (canal_wall material, a quay_floor top) with m.taken over it so ribs and stubs keep clear; m.no_bank under it; handrail04_long props every 128 along its open edge when it is over 64 up; a ladder (m.ladder, pieces of 128) or m.steps to reach it.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** canal, valve, layout

- **See:** `valve_canals/02_city_canal_walk_section.png`, `valve_canals/07_city_canal_iso.png`, `valve_canals/08_city_canal_iso.png`, `valve_canals/18_reservoir_water_iso.png`

### Drain outfalls: big round holes in a ring (`valve-canal-outfalls`)

An outfall in a canal wall is a round hole 128 across running deep into the wall (Valve: 588 deep, 420 to 966), framed by the canal_cap001 ring (154 across), its middle about 139 over the floor (93 to 172), in groups about 384 apart (288 to 768), sometimes a pair either side of a wall's middle; a concrete drain stain (decalconcretedrain002a) under each.

- **Why:** 65 canal_cap001 placed across the 13 maps, 58 with their holes measured. Our drain stubs are 48 across at 176 up, one per wall per 1024: too small and too even. Route Kanal pictures (_dev/reference/valve_canals/) R13.
- **How:** Channel.dress's stubs: a hole 128 across (an octagon prism of black inside, as now, 40 to 64 deep is enough to read) with canal_cap001 on the wall face, centre at F + 140; place them in twos or threes 384 apart rather than one every 1024; keep the drain decal under each.
- **Checked by:** map_assets.py (canal_cap001 is a static model: check it passes), then review
- **Tags:** canal, valve, props

- **See:** `valve_canals/13_outfalls_eye.png`

### Canal lamps every 600 to 950, each with its own light (`valve-canal-lamps`)

Valve hangs light_cagelight02_on on the canal walls about 811 apart (581 to 956), 311 over the canal floor (233 to 342), and gives every lit one its own light within 96 (brightness about 70). Ours are 1536 apart at 260 up: halve the spacing in long stretches and lift them, keeping the dead ones and the dark gaps the pools need.

- **Why:** 100 cage lamps measured in the 13 maps; 99 have a light within 96. The whole of Route Kanal has only 208 light, 315 light_spot and 68 point_spotlight: few lights, but on a steady rhythm along the water. Route Kanal pictures (_dev/reference/valve_canals/) R12.
- **How:** dress_canal_walls: a lamp on every other rib (1024) or on ribs set 768 apart, at F + 300, about one in four dead; the light 30 out from the wall, brightness 60 to 80.
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py offlamp (an off lamp has no light), then review
- **Tags:** canal, valve, lighting

- **See:** `valve_canals/12_culvert_inside_eye.png`

### The canal's top fence is mostly fence props (`valve-canal-fence-props`)

Fence the canal's wall tops with Valve's fence props end to end rather than brush sheets: fence03a (108 high, 260 long) or exterior_fence002e (152 high, 512 long), feet on the wall top; canal_bridge_railing01 or 02 (32 to 40 high) on bridges. Ask the owner before changing the fence they already have (they asked for fencing along the top).

- **Why:** Valve placed 190 fence03a (258 apart: end to end) and 171 exterior_fence002e on canal tops; its brush rails (metalrail013a/b/c, 1,292 faces) are railings on walks and stairs, and brush chain link (metalfence004a, 205 faces) is rare. The house lesson props-not-brushes says the same. Ours is a brush chain link (metalfence007a).
- **How:** map_build.fence: along each run, a fence03a every 260 (or exterior_fence002e every 512) at the wall's top, yawed along the run; posts come with the model.
- **Checked by:** map_assets.py, map_floating.py (each fence touches the wall top), then review
- **Tags:** canal, valve, props

### Culverts and pipes in Valve's sizes (`valve-canal-culverts`)

Culverts off a canal: box culverts 512 wide by 224 to 256 high (our gate is already this) with their floors dished 64 into the walls; round pipes 344 to 384 across; arches 320 wide and 256 to 352 high; big box tunnels 704 by 288 to 352. A culvert's mouth is a hole 252 to 260 high in a wall 48 to 80 thick; small openings are closed by canal_bars002 (128 wide, 124 high).

- **Why:** 128 tunnel cuts across the 13 maps: round pipes 384 across the commonest (19), then box culverts 512 by 256 (9). Route Kanal pictures (_dev/reference/valve_canals/) R6, R9, R12.
- **How:** New tunnel or drain modules: use these sizes; round pipes as octagon or 16-sided prisms (true-curves), bars as canal_bars002 props, a little water or slime only where the owner's sewers join (canal-water).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** canal, valve, geometry

- **See:** `valve_canals/06_culverts_section.png`, `valve_canals/09_airboat_canal_iso.png`, `valve_canals/10_city_canal_eye.png`, `valve_canals/12_culvert_inside_eye.png`

### Vary the canal between its ends (`valve-canal-varies`)

Valve's canal changes its cross-section from stretch to stretch: 512 (city) and 1024 (wide canals) are the commonest widths, but cuts run from 320 to 1216, walls step between 256 and 512 high, and in d1_canals_01 a narrow wet channel (432 wide, 188 deep) runs inside a wider dry one. Modules must start and end on our plain section (512, floor F), but in their middle they may widen into a basin, step a wall back to a ledge, or sink a narrower channel in the floor.

- **Why:** 372 open cuts: width 128 up 1024 in 70, 896 in 23, 832 in 21, 512 in 20, 704 in 19; wall tops 512 in 68 and 256 in 64. Route Kanal pictures (_dev/reference/valve_canals/) R2, R3, R4, R7, R9, R11.
- **How:** m.open with a wider v range in the middle of a module (side rooms go out in +v), m.low for a stepped wall, m.water or a lower floor for a sunken channel; join back to v -256 to 256 before u L.
- **Checked by:** map_modules_check.py (the ends still match), then review
- **Tags:** canal, valve, layout

- **See:** `valve_canals/02_city_canal_walk_section.png`, `valve_canals/03_airboat_canal_section.png`, `valve_canals/04_battered_canal_section.png`, `valve_canals/07_city_canal_iso.png`, `valve_canals/09_airboat_canal_iso.png`, `valve_canals/11_airboat_canal_eye.png`, `valve_canals/14_bridge_eye.png`

### A lock is Valve's lock gates in a recess (`valve-canal-locks`)

A lock across a canal is a pair of Valve's gate props, locks_large and locks_large_b (each leaf about 205 thick, 446 wide, 572 high), set 441 apart so the pair closes about 885, standing in recesses of the walls; the next pair stands 5,185 to 7,300 down the canal.

- **Why:** Eight pairs measured in d1_canals_08 (3), 12 (3) and 13 (2). Route Kanal pictures (_dev/reference/valve_canals/) R15, R18.
- **How:** m.static or m.dynamic (named, to open) for each leaf; widen the canal to about 896 at the gates with m.open, or use one leaf in our 512 canal; m.taken round the gates.
- **Checked by:** map_assets.py, then review
- **Tags:** canal, valve, props

- **See:** `valve_canals/15_lock_iso.png`, `valve_canals/18_reservoir_water_iso.png`

### A valve wheel moves the gate as far as it is turned (`valve-wheel-gate`)

A wheel players turn is a momentary_rot_button: an unseen toolsinvisible brush, spawnflags 1024 (use) + 64 or 128 (the axis, none for z) + 1 (not solid), distance 210 to 800 (negative turns the other way), speed 18 to 180, returnspeed 30 to 240, sounds 24, with the wheel model a prop_dynamic parented to it. Its Position output goes to SetPosition on a func_movelinear (the gate or lift), so the gate follows the wheel; OnFullyClosed, once, plays the lock sound and Locks the wheel. A second wheel without the use flag can follow the first by the same SetPosition. When the game opens the gate itself, the same relay sends the wheel SetPosition and Lock so the wheel shows the gate's state.

- **Why:** 8 momentary_rot_button in 4 canal maps, every one with its model parented. d1_canals_05 gate3_wheel (props_pipes/valvewheel002): Position -> door_lock2_2.SetPosition (func_movelinear, movedistance 148, startsound d1_canals.citizen_gate.Move1, stopsound d1_canals.citizen_gate.FullClose1), OnFullyClosed -> canals_sound_lock01 (d1_canals.citizen_gate.WheelLock1, flags 48) PlaySound x1 and gate3_wheel Lock x1. Its Position also turns rotate_exitgate_wheel and _wheel2 (spawnflags 65: no use flag). d1_canals_10 gate4_wheel (props_borealis/door_wheel001a) lifts canals_brush_boathouselift1 320. d1_canals_11 relay_guncave_gateopen: door Open, wheel Unlock, wheel SetPosition 1 at 0.1. Our lc_stash_valve and lc_aqueduct_valve fire once on a press.
- **How:** A helper m.wheel(name, u, v, z, axis, drives, distance) that makes the brush, the parented model, the func_movelinear wiring and a once-only lock sound; lc_NAME_open / _shut relays also move the wheel.
- **Checked by:** review; proposed map_lint wire: a momentary_rot_button needs an axis flag (or turns about z), a distance, and a Position line that reaches a SetPosition.
- **Tags:** logic, valve, entities, canal

### An areaportal in a doorway shuts with its door, never by a trigger (`valve-portal-door`)

A portal where a door hangs names the door in its target key (prop_door_rotating, func_door); for a gate that is a func_door_rotating or func_movelinear, the gate's OnOpen sends the portal Open and its OnFullyClosed sends Close. It starts closed (StartOpen 0) when the door starts shut. Never open or close a portal from a trigger in a map many people share: Valve's triggers open the way ahead and shut it behind one player, which would blind everyone behind him.

- **Why:** 173 func_areaportal in 18 maps: 93 have no name and only cut what is drawn; 11 name their door in target (10 prop_door_rotating such as d1_trainstation_01 barney_door_1, 1 func_door, d1_trainstation_05 lab_door); 71 output lines open or close the rest, 62 of them from triggers (d1_trainstation_03 portal_courtyard_doorway1: two strips 60 apart, Open then Close). d1_canals_08: door_locke1_1 OnFullyClosed -> relay_locks_closegateportal -> portal_locks_1 Close.
- **How:** map_build.areaportal(box, vis, name=None, door=None) writes the target key, or the door's OnOpen / OnFullyClosed wires; today our portals never close. City 17 wires occluders the same way: d3_c17_10a bldg1_door2 and soldierscare_door OnOpen send their occluder Deactivate and OnFullyClosed Activate; d3_c17_05 barricade_1_gate_open_rl Deactivates backstreet_gate_occluder and the close relay Activates it 2 s later; 42 of 46 func_occluder start active.
- **Checked by:** review; proposed: a portal whose box meets a door's box with neither target nor door wiring; any trigger that sends a portal Open or Close.
- **Tags:** logic, valve, performance, doors

### A sound waits silent and plays once unless it loops (`valve-sound-flags`)

A sound the wiring plays is an ambient_generic with spawnflags 48 (starts silent, not looped) for a one-shot, 16 for a loop that waits for PlaySound and StopSound, 0 only for a loop that runs from the start (49 adds 'play everywhere'). Name the game's sound scripts (Doors.Move*, Buttons.snd*, d1_canals.*) rather than a raw .wav; volume (health) 10, radius about 1250, 2000 for a blast, 10000 for a siren. Of two leaves that move together, only one carries the move sound.

- **Why:** 419 ambient_generic: 407 name a sound script, 12 a .wav; spawnflags 48 on 240, 16 on 68, 49 on 59, 0 on 34; health 10 on 377; radius 1250 on 223. d1_canals_05 canals_sound_lock01 (48), d1_canals_07 sound_redlight1 d1_canals.Floodgate_AlarmBellLoop (16), d2_coast_12 bunker_1_alarm_1 coast.bunker_siren1 (16, radius 10000); d1_canals_08 door_locke1_1 has noise1/noise2, its twin door_locke1_2 none. Our m.sound uses health 7, radius 1100, spawnflags 0 or 16 and never 32 (what GMod then does with a one-shot fired twice is unchecked).
- **How:** m.sound(..., once=True) gives 48; the default stays a loop.
- **Checked by:** review; proposed: a sound that outputs PlaySound but lacks flag 16 (it also plays at spawn).
- **Tags:** logic, valve, sound

### A timer sleeps until someone is there (`valve-timer-asleep`)

A logic_timer that only dresses a place (dust, sparks, steam, a far noise) starts disabled, with a random time (UseRandomTime 1, bounds such as 1 to 8), and is enabled by a trigger_multiple when the first player comes in (OnStartTouchAll Enable) and disabled when the last leaves (OnEndTouchAll Disable). A timer the gamemode needs (a train timetable) may run always.

- **Why:** 48 of 50 logic_timer start disabled and 36 use random times (bounds 1-8, 1-3, 3-5 commonest); they are enabled by triggers and relays and Killed when done: d1_canals_03 deadly_steamjet_1_timer (3 to 5 s), d3_c17_13 transition_flickerlight_timer (Enable and Disable from trigger_multiple), d2_coast_12 bunker suppression timers. Ours (lc_ash_timer, lc_underpass_dust_timer, lc_thumper_timer) run from the start on a server that runs all day.
- **How:** m.timer(name, u, v, z, lo, hi, wires, area=box) builds the timer off and the presence trigger round the place.
- **Checked by:** review; proposed: a timer that starts enabled and is not on the gamemode's list.
- **Tags:** logic, valve, performance

### 'When all of these have happened' is a math_counter (`valve-counter`)

Each event sends Add 1 to a math_counter whose max is how many; OnHitMax fires the result. An AND of two different things (a scene ended and an item taken) is a counter with max 2. A wave that comes again resets its own counter from OnHitMax (SetValue 0). Every counter needs at least max feeders.

- **Why:** 55 math_counter in 16 maps, max 2 on 17 and 3 on 13; fed by NPC OnDeath (55 lines), prop OnBreak (16), path_track OnPass (11). d1_canals_01 traintrackcops_south_assault_counter: cop OnDeath Add; OnHitMax -> traintrackcops_south_makers_relay Enable and SetValue 0 on itself. d1_trainstation_05 get_suit_math_1 (max 2): lab03_lcs OnCompletion and item_suit OnPlayerTouch. d1_canals_01a sewerbarrels_counter (max 4): barrels OnBreak, then sewerbarrels_gate_intact Kill and sewerbarrels_gate_broken Enable. Valve shipped counters nothing adds to (d1_canals_01a train_boardbreak_counter1 and 2).
- **How:** m.counter(name, u, v, z, top, done_wires, again=False); our lc_nest_count and lc_armory_count already work this way. d3_c17_10b switches_counter (max 3): each control room's relay (force field Kill, generator off, sparks, shake) Adds 1; OnHitMax fires one success relay that ends the whole stronghold's defences, the shape of our administrator's last stand (collapse.md).
- **Checked by:** review; proposed: a counter with fewer Add lines than its max.
- **Tags:** logic, valve

### One relay per event, its steps timed by delays (`valve-relay-per-event`)

Each thing the gamemode or a player sets off is one logic_relay whose outputs are staggered (the sound first, the move 0.1 to 2 seconds later, the end a few seconds after); a second relay only for what also happens alone (stop, reset). Relays come in pairs (open and shut, start and stop) and the gamemode fires only relays, never the moving parts.

- **Why:** 414 logic_relay, 2056 outputs (median 3, about 12 at the 90th percentile, most 42 in d1_trainstation_01 scene4_start); of 344 relays called from outside, 280 call no other relay; the longest delay in a relay is 0.2 s at the median and 2 s at the upper quarter. Pairs: d1_canals_07 logic_start_redlight / logic_stop_redlight; d1_canals_08 relay_locks_opengates / relay_locks_closegates (Unlock at 0, Open at 0.1, klaxon at 0, 1, 2 and 3). Relays drive doors more than anything else does (func_door: Open 11, Close 5; func_door_rotating: Open 9, Toggle 7, Close 6, Unlock 5).
- **How:** Keep map_raven's and map_forest's lc_X_open / _shut / _opened / _closed relays for every moving thing the game uses; m.relay as it is. A relay whose steps are spread over time is stopped with CancelPending when its event ends early: 19 lines in City 17, 17 of them in d3_c17_07's waves (northfront_battle_reset CancelPending on wave relays 1 to 5) and d3_c17_03's nags; Kill is the commonest input in the 15 maps (532 of 5008 lines): what has done its job is removed.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** logic, valve

### A switch shows its state on itself (`valve-switch-state`)

A switch shows on or off with its own model (CombineButton.mdl SetAnimation togglein / toggleout) and a small sprite whose colour changes (0 255 0 on, 255 0 0 off), not with text and not by switching room lights. It Locks as it moves and Unlocks a second later, so a double press does not undo it.

- **Why:** d2_coast_12 thumper_1_button: func_door spawnflags 289 (use opens, toggle), wait 3, noise2 Buttons.snd9; OnOpen: thumper_1_button_model togglein, thumper_1_button_sprite (sprites/glow01.spr, scale 0.1) Color 0 255 0 at 0.2 and SetScale 0.05 then 0.1, thumper_1 Enable, Lock at 0, Unlock at 1; OnClose the reverse. Valve switches or recolours 67 env_sprite but switches only 23 of its 948 lights; 14 env_hudhint and 1 game_text in 23 maps (house rule 10).
- **How:** m.switch(name, u, v, z, out, on_wires, off_wires) built as the coast's; our wall_button could carry such a sprite. Recipe, as Valve wires it (the owner asked to keep it, 21 November): R3 Toggle switch with status light (d2_coast_12 thumper_1_button, alarm_button): func_door 20x10x12 `spawnflags` 288 or 289 (use opens + toggle), `wait` 3, `noise2` Buttons.snd9; prop_dynamic props_combine/CombineButton.mdl; env_sprite sprites/glow01.spr (`rendermode` 5, `scale` 0.1, red or green); optional env_lightglow. `OnOpen`: `model.SetAnimation(togglein)`, `sprite.Color(0 255 0) @0.2`, `sprite.SetScale(0.05) @0.2`, `SetScale(0.1) @0.3`, `machine.Enable @0.2`, `!self.Lock @0`, `!self.Unlock @1`. `OnClose`: the same with toggleout, 255 0 0, Disable.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** logic, valve, gameplay, recipe

### Breaking a place swaps intact for broken in one go (`valve-swap-broken`)

A wall, door or floor that gets blown or breached is two named sets: the intact props and brushes (Kill or Break) and the broken ones (prop_dynamic and func_brush with StartDisabled 1: TurnOn or Enable). One relay does it with a one-shot sound (48, radius 2000), env_shake (amplitude 12, radius 1000, 1 s, frequency 2.5), env_explosion (magnitude 100, flag 1 for no damage) and fire half a second later.

- **Why:** d3_c17_13 bigdestroy1_relay: bigdestroy1_props_intact (3 prop_dynamic) Kill, bigdestroy1_props_broken (8 prop_dynamic, StartDisabled 1) TurnOn, bigdestroy1_breakables_intact Break, bigdestroy1_explosion Explode, bigdestroy1_shake StartShake, bigdestroy1_wav (Streetwar.d3_C17_13_explode_2) PlaySound, bigdestroy1_fire StartFire at 0.5; bigdestroy3 also wakes a prop_physics beam and fires env_physexplosion at 0.5. d1_canals_01a: sewerbarrels_gate_intact Kill, _broken Enable.
- **How:** m.swap(name, intact, broken, boom=True) giving lc_NAME_break, the relay the gamemode fires (a Combine breach, a collapse). Recipe, as Valve wires it (the owner asked to keep it, 21 November): R12 Breaking things (lesson valve-swap-broken): env_explosion `iMagnitude` 100, `iRadiusOverride` 0, `fireballsprite` sprites/zerogxplode.spr, `rendermode` 5, flag 1 no damage (19 of 46). env_shake `amplitude` 12, `radius` 1000, `duration` 1, `frequency` 2.5 (commonest; 20 fired from path_track OnPass, 12 from relays). env_physexplosion `spawnflags` 1, `magnitude` 50, `radius` 0 (or 128 to 200 with a named target). env_spark `magnitude` 1 to 2, `traillength` 1 to 3, `spawnflags` 128 or 640; driven StartSpark / SparkOnce / StopSpark. env_fire: burning from the start `spawnflags` 15 or 47 (4 start on, 8 full), `health` 30 to 300, `firesize` 48 to 130. func_breakable boards: `material` 1 wood (243 of 349), `health` 1 to 10, `spawnflags` 256; func_physbox freed by relays (`EnableMotion` 154 lines, `Break` 62, `Wake` 24). d3_c17_12 strider breach: a trigger_once sends each strider SetCannonTarget at a named npc_bullseye (health 20, damagefilter strider_only_filter); its OnDeath disables destroyed_wall_1_brush_1, turns on destroyed_wall_1_hole_1, explodes @0 and @0.5, shoots concrete from an env_shooter, shakes, ForceSpawns a debris template and turns on destroyed_wall_1_link (the hole becomes a path).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** logic, valve, props, recipe

- **See:** `valve_c17/31_cratered_park_eye.png`

### A hazard that comes and goes is a timer, a relay and switched parts (`valve-hazard-cycle`)

Steam or sparks that hurt on a rhythm: a logic_timer (random 3 to 5 s, off until players are near) triggers a relay that turns on the effect, Enables a trigger_hurt and a trigger_push and plays a one-shot sound, and turns all of them off 1.7 s later. Everything starts off.

- **Why:** d1_canals_03 deadly_steamjet_1_timer -> deadly_steamjet_1_on_rl: env_steam deadly_steamjet_1 TurnOn (speed 180, startsize 25, endsize 40, rate 40, jetlength 80), deadly_steamjet_1_hurt Enable (damage 20, damagetype 8), deadly_steamjet_1_push Enable (speed 40), underground_steamjet (48); all off at 1.7. Three of them in a row make a run to time (a challenge, not a wait: house rule 9).
- **How:** m.hazard(name, box, kind='steam') in the sewers and works.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** logic, valve, gameplay

### Every trigger says who touches it, and counts many players (`valve-trigger-who`)

Set spawnflags on every trigger: 1 players, 2 NPCs, 8 physics props, 64 everything; add a filter only to pick one kind or name. trigger_multiple waits 1 second. On a shared server: trigger_once fires for the first player only (once a round); OnStartTouchAll and OnEndTouchAll mean 'someone is here' and 'all have gone' (a plain OnEndTouch from one player undoes it for the others); !activator is 'whoever did it'; never !player (in multiplayer it is always player 1).

- **Why:** 664 of 808 trigger_once and trigger_multiple use flag 1, 75 a filter (an NPC class or name, the airboat); trigger_multiple wait 1 on 199 of 305; 92 triggers start disabled until the story opens them. !activator in 93 outputs, !player in 11 and in 52 aiscripted_schedule goals. d1_town_03 street_lever_unlock_trigger: OnStartTouch Unlock, OnEndTouch Lock (the lever works only from the street side); d1_canals_07 trigger_room5_detect_desk_movement OnEndTouchAll.
- **How:** m.ent('trigger_multiple', ...) with flags always given; use the All outputs for presence.
- **Checked by:** review; proposed: a trigger with spawnflags 0, any !player, an OnEndTouch that undoes an OnStartTouch.
- **Tags:** logic, valve, multiplayer

### Group by one name, leave no loose ends (`valve-names-no-loose-ends`)

Things that switch together share one targetname and one output reaches them all (Valve's way), or carry a numbered stem reached by one wildcard (ours, lc_swing_lamp_*); wildcards are otherwise rare. Every output names something that is in the map or that the gamemode makes: the game says nothing when it is not. Parent one level deep, two at most.

- **Why:** 482 targetnames are shared (68 groups of prop_physics, 44 of ambient_generic, 17 of func_areaportal, d1_trainstation_04 portal_endsection x4); 30 of 7711 outputs use a wildcard. 98 outputs in Valve's own maps name nothing: d1_canals_01 boards send Add 46 times to train_boardbreak_counter1 and 2, which live in d1_canals_01a; d1_trainstation_04 citizen_DoorBracer and door_bracerProp. Parent depth: 437 children one level, 41 two, 1 three. Our names check calls a name used twice a PROBLEM: a shared name inside one module would need allowing.
- **How:** Keep lc_ names; list what the gamemode spawns so the check knows them.
- **Checked by:** proposed map_lint wire: output targets that do not exist, wildcards that match nothing, parentname that does not exist, filtername that is not a filter.
- **Tags:** logic, valve, check

### A door the map closes tries again when blocked (`valve-door-retry`)

A door closed by the wiring sends itself Close again from OnBlockedClosing a second later, and Locks from OnFullyClosed rather than with the Close (a player in the way would otherwise leave it open and locked). Heavy doors that must never stop keep the crusher (map_forest.crusher).

- **Why:** d1_trainstation_01 barney_door_2 (prop_door_rotating, spawnflags 34816): OnBlockedClosing -> barney_door_2 Close at 1, OnFullyClosed -> Lock; introom_door_1 the same; barney_door_close_trigger sends Close at 0 and Lock at 0.1. On a busy server players stand in doorways all the time.
- **How:** m.door and the door helpers add the two wires when the door is closed by the map. Recipe, as Valve wires it (the owner asked to keep it, 21 November): R5 Door the map closes (d1_trainstation_01 barney_door_2, introom_door_1): `door.Close @0`, `door.Lock @0.1`; on the door `OnBlockedClosing > !self.Close @1`, `OnFullyClosed > !self.Lock`. prop_door_rotating `spawnflags` 8192 normal (23 of 51), 2048 starts locked, 32768 ignores players' use; `hardware` 1, `distance` 90, `speed` 100, `returndelay` -1.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** logic, valve, doors, multiplayer, recipe

### An alarm is a turning red light, a siren and makers, on two relays (`valve-alarm`)

A start relay turns on a red beacon (func_rotating carrying two red point_spotlights back to back, in a caged lamp), plays a siren or bell loop (16) a moment later and Spawns from disabled npc_template_makers; a stop relay (fired by the gamemode, or by the start relay after a set time) turns all of it off. A one-shot klaxon (48) can be fired several times a second apart instead of a loop.

- **Why:** d1_canals_07 logic_start_redlight: spot_doors and spot_doors2 LightOn (point_spotlight 255 0 0, length 96, width 25, spawnflags 2, angles 0 0 -180 and 0 180 -180, parented to rot_redlight1), rot_redlight1 Start (func_rotating 16 cube, spawnflags 64, maxspeed 350, fanfriction 20) inside props_c17/light_cagelight01_on, sound_redlight1 PlaySound at 2 and StopSound at 14, logic_stop_redlight at 120. d2_coast_12 bunker_1_alarm_relay: coast.bunker_siren1 and 2 (16, radius 10000) and a red glow sprite. d1_canals_08 klaxon at 0, 1, 2, 3. 194 of 197 npc_template_maker start disabled and are fired by Spawn.
- **How:** m.alarm(name, u, v, z, makers=()) giving lc_NAME_alarm_on / _off for the gamemode (a raid on a checkpoint). Recipe, as Valve wires it (the owner asked to keep it, 21 November): R7 Alarm (d1_canals_07 logic_start_redlight, d2_coast_12 bunker_1_alarm_relay, d1_canals_08 relay_locks_opengates): Beacon: func_rotating 16 cube nodraw, `spawnflags` 64, `maxspeed` 350, `fanfriction` 20; two point_spotlight parented to it, `angles` 0 0 -180 and 0 180 -180, `rendercolor` 255 0 0, `spotlightlength` 96, `spotlightwidth` 25, `spawnflags` 2, `HDRColorScale` 0.6; inside props_c17/light_cagelight01_on. Start relay: `spot.LightOn`, `rot.Start`, `bell.PlaySound @2`, `bell.StopSound @14`, `stop_relay.Trigger @120`. Stop relay: `LightOff`, `rot.Stop`. Sirens coast.bunker_siren1/2 (16, radius 10000) or a one-shot klaxon d1_canals.Floodgate_Klaxon (48) fired `@0, @1, @2, @3`. Makers: npc_template_maker `StartDisabled` 1, `MaxNPCCount` 1, `MaxLiveChildren` 1 to 5, `SpawnFrequency` 5, `Radius` 256, `CriterionVisibility` 2, `CriterionDistance` 2; fired by `Spawn` from triggers and relays (100 lines) or `Enable`; `OnSpawnNPC > schedule.StartSchedule` (aiscripted_schedule, goal an info_target, never !player).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** logic, valve, combine, sound, recipe

### Small things fade far off; cover never does (`valve-fade-clutter`)

Outdoors, give small clutter a fade, the start about 80 percent of the end: shrubs, rubbish and handrails 2000 to 2500, fences, driftwood and containers 3000 to 5000, poles, barriers and tree cards 10000 to 12000. Never fade what players hide behind (a far player would see through it); rooms behind areaportals need no fade.

- **Why:** 2363 of 6970 prop_static and 711 of 2237 prop_physics carry a fade: shrub_01a 2000 to 2500, garbage256_composite002b 2000 to 2200, exterior_fence002e 3000 to 4000, cargo_container01 3500 to 3800, utilitypole02b and concrete_barrier001a 10000 to 12000. Our m.static never fades.
- **How:** m.static(..., fade=(2000, 2500)) for clutter in open modules.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** logic, valve, performance, props, multiplayer

### Loose things turned, fixed things square (`valve-turn-the-loose`)

Turn loose things off the grid and keep fixed things square. Of Valve's physics props only 41% sit on exact 0/90/180/270; 34% are turned 1 to 20 degrees and 21% at any angle. Crates are square only 19% of the time, trash 27%, push carts 14%, couches 15%, chairs 32%, barrels 57%. Fixed things are square: radiators and payphones 100%, benches 89%, fences 88%, prop_dynamic 89%, prop_static 69%.

- **Why:** Measured on 8,951 props in 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November): 2,193 prop_physics, 6,154 prop_static. Our scratch town build had 93% of all props on exact 90s and 26 of its 42 physics props square. d3_c17_01 to 12b (21 November): physics props on exact 90s 54%, turned 3 to 20 23%, free 20%; tilted 28% (lying 13%); statics on 90s 81%; cars only 15% square.
- **How:** In Mod.loose (and any scatter): yaw = the square yaw plus a turn drawn from -20..20 for crates, boxes, barrels, trash, carts, chairs and suitcases, and one in five at a free angle; keep wall furniture, benches, radiators, phones and fences on the grid. Crates in a group are never all square (6% of Valve's crate groups are).
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint warns when more than 60% of a module's prop_physics sit on exact 90s
- **Tags:** props, valve

### Some loose things lie down (`valve-tilt-and-topple`)

Lay a share of loose things on their side or upside down, and let things on rough ground lean a little. Valve: 43% of physics props have pitch or roll, 23% lie on their side (75 to 105), 6% upside down, 7% lean under 15. By kind: trash 53% lying, cinderblocks 53% (16% upside down), suitcases 47%, pallets 39% lying and 13% upside down, barrels 28% (41% of barrel groups have one down), buckets 21%; crates 6%, boxes 6%, tables 5% stay upright. On displacement ground 54% of dressing props are tilted, 14% by a small slope (15 or less), against 26% and 3% on flat floors.

- **Why:** Measured in 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November). Our helper Mod.prop writes angles '0 yaw 0' only, so nothing we place can lean or lie.
- **How:** Let Mod.prop take pitch and roll. For loose clutter: one barrel in three on its side (roll 90, raise z to the drum's radius), half the bottles, cans and cartons on their side, pallets on edge against a wall (pitch 70 to 85) or flat, cinderblocks on any face. On a displacement, tilt the prop by the ground's slope (2 to 15). The mattress keeps its own lesson (mattress-turned).
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint notes a module with 10 or more loose props and none tilted; map_floating.py still catches anything sunk or floating
- **Tags:** props, valve

- **See:** `valve_c17/35_rubble_corner_shop_iso.png`

### Loose props asleep, junk as debris, fixtures held still (`valve-physics-flags`)

Set the physics flags as Valve does: start asleep (1) on barrels, crates and trash that wait for the player (26% of physics props); debris (4) on small junk so it never blocks a player (10%: trash 99, boards 38, cans 36, boxes 12); motion disabled (8) on things that must stay until hit (29%: nailed boards 207, dock planks 61, barrels 55, crates 37, radiators 24 of 25, shelves 21, tables 12, couches 11, signs 16), with 64 (free on a gravity-gun grab) on 76 of them. 256 (output on use) is Hammer's default and on most.

- **Why:** prop_physics spawnflags across 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November): bits 0:571, 2:219, 3:637, 6:76, 7:254 (rotor wash, canals), 8:694. Valve placed only 3 prop_dynamic_override, 9 prop_physics_override and no prop_physics_multiplayer in 22 maps; standing physics furniture is prop_physics with flag 8. Our Mod.loose writes spawnflags 0 on everything. In the uprising's battle maps (d3_c17_01 to 12b, 748 physics props) loose things are mostly left awake: asleep 9%, motion disabled 21% (shelves, boards, rubble, radiators, fences, cars), debris 4% (trash), flags 0 on 119; physdamagescale 0.1 kept on 744.
- **How:** Mod.loose(..., asleep=True) writing spawnflags 1 by default; 5 (asleep + debris) for trash, cans, bottles, papers, shoes; boards nailed over openings 8. Whether standing furniture should become prop_physics with flag 8 instead of prop_dynamic_override (physics-props-dynamic) is the owner's call: ask first.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint lists prop_physics with spawnflags 0 (none asleep) and small junk without the debris flag
- **Tags:** props, valve, performance

### Small things fade out (`valve-fade-small`)

Give small props a fade distance: about a third of Valve's props have one (statics 35 to 41% of medium and large models, physics 26 to 42%), rarely dynamics (8%). Typical min/max: small loose junk, chairs and corpses 1000/1200 to 1024/1500; boards and cinderblocks 1200/1600; crates 1900/2500; barrels 2000/3000; debris 1800/2000; fences 2750/3200; large statics 2500/3000; huge 4000/5000; props seen across open water 10000/12000. fadescale 1 (97%).

- **Why:** fademindist and fademaxdist on 8,951 props in 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November); commonest pairs 10000/12000 (217), 3000/3200 (214), 10000/11000 (184), 2000/2500 (167), 4000/5000 (145), 1000/1200 (143). Our Mod.static, loose and dynamic write fademaxdist 0 on everything. d3_c17_01 to 12b set fades on fewer props (physics 12%, statics 16%): rubble 1200, lamps 1200, Combine pieces 1100, fences 2400, barricades 2600, bins 2250, crates 2200, bottles 1100.
- **How:** A size table in Mod.prop: under 24 tall 1000/1500, under 64 1500/2000, crates and barrels 2000/2500 to 3000, fences 2750/3200; leave big architecture props and anything seen from far without a fade.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint counts loose props under 64 with no fade in an outdoor module
- **Tags:** props, valve, performance

### No shadow from flat things, no collision on what nobody touches (`valve-shadows-and-solid`)

Switch shadows off on flat and wall-mounted props and collisions off on soft or unreachable ones. Valve's disableshadows 1: lamps 52%, wall boxes and machines 49%, ladders 55%, fences 45%, nailed boards 45%, trash 43%, prop_dynamic 67%, all statics 26%. Non-solid statics (solid 0) are 22%: shrubs and ivy (55% of plants), flat garbage piles (86% of static trash), canal caps, pipe brackets, pole connectors, power towers, lampposts out of reach (66%) and signs (64%).

- **Why:** Counted on 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November): 1,335 of 6,154 statics solid 0 (shrub_01a 190, ivy_01 110, cattails 62, garbage256_composite002a/b 98); 1,609 statics, 369 physics, 282 dynamics disableshadows 1. Our scratch town: 1 non-solid static in 390.
- **How:** Mod.static(..., solid=False) for foliage, garbage piles and anything above head height or behind bars; disableshadows 1 for lamps, wall boxes, signs, ladders, fences and boards.
- **Checked by:** review; proposed: map_lint notes shrubs, ivy and garbage piles placed solid
- **Tags:** props, valve, performance

### Use the skins a model has (`valve-skin-variety`)

Draw skins at random on models that have them, and never tint props. 9% of Valve's props carry a skin other than 0, all on models built with skins: oildrum001 45% (skins 1 and 2), cargo_container01 69% (0, 1, 2 about evenly), wood_crate001a 33%, plasticcrate01a 60%, door01_left 91% (7 the commonest), lights_florescent01a 88% skin 1 (lit), light_spotlight01_base 94% skin 1. In barrel groups of plain drums, 51% mix skins. rendercolor is never changed on a static or physics prop (4 dark dynamics only); no modelscale anywhere.

- **Why:** skin, rendercolor and modelscale keys on 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November). Our scratch town: 15 of 693 props with a skin other than 0.
- **How:** Mod.prop(..., skin=None) drawing from the model's skins (map_assets can read $texturegroup from the QC) for drums, containers, crates, plastic crates and doors; skin 1 on fluorescent tubes and spotlight bases that are lit.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint notes 4 or more of one multi-skin model all on skin 0
- **Tags:** props, valve

### Clutter in small groups of one kind, with an odd one (`valve-prop-groups`)

Place clutter as groups of 2 or 3 of one kind, sometimes with one partner thing, not as single scattered props. Within 96: barrels have another barrel 65% of the time, crates 66%, boxes 67%, cans 76%, boards 87%, shelves 75%. Barrel groups: 156 alone, 72 pairs, 19 threes, 18 of four or five, nearest neighbour 31 to 58 (median 39, about touching). Crate groups: 98 alone, 36 pairs, 24 threes, 8 fours. Commonest mixed groups: barrel+crate (24 clusters in 10 maps), crate+trash (16 in 9), crate+pallet (14 in 11), chair+table (14 in 10), bench+trash (14), bin+trash (13), corpse+crate (11 in 9), barrel+pallet (11 in 8).

- **Why:** Single-link clusters at 96 across 3,307 dressing props in 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November); 540 clusters, sizes 2 (median) to 7 (90th percentile). A lamppost is not part of a group: 34 of 38 lampposts (lamppost03a_off) have no dressing prop within 192; benches come in rows instead (2 to 6, 96 to 112 apart, 83% the same way, 62% against a wall) with trash within 70 (45%), a payphone (20%) or a bin (18%).
- **How:** A Mod.group(kind, n, partner=None) helper: n of 2 or 3, spaced 34 to 44 for drums, touching or stacked for crates, each turned (valve-turn-the-loose), then maybe one partner (barrel: crate or pallet; crate: pallet or trash; dumpster: trash bags; bench: trash; table: one to three chairs).
- **Checked by:** review (the groups show in the viewer); proposed: map_lint notes a module whose loose props are all more than 96 from each other
- **Tags:** props, valve

### Stacks are never neat columns (`valve-stacks`)

Stack things the way people drop them. 17% of Valve's crates stand on another crate, the top one turned (median 7 degrees, a quarter over 30) and shifted (median 8, a quarter over 20) from the one under it; tyres lie flat (76%) in columns of 2 to 6 (44% stacked); physics props stand on another prop 14% of the time, statics 4%. Small things stand on furniture: TVs on shelves (9), papers and binders on desks, pots on the stove (7 to 12 up, 13 to 21 from its middle).

- **Why:** Prop-on-prop contacts (bottom within 4 of another prop's top, 30% footprint overlap) in 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November): crate on crate 49, tyre on tyre 36, board on board 28, barrel on barrel 15.
- **How:** When stacking: z on the lower prop's top (map_assets.size), yaw plus -30..30, shift 2 to 20 inside the lower top; tyre stacks of 2 to 4 lying flat, each turned freely.
- **Checked by:** map_floating.py (nothing floats); proposed: map_lint notes stacked props with exactly the same place and yaw
- **Tags:** props, valve

### A few busy spots, much clear floor (`valve-busy-and-quiet`)

Concentrate props in one to three busy spots per place and keep the rest clear. In 256 x 256 squares of walkable floor most hold nothing (indoor city 47%, outdoor 74%, canals 65 to 79%) and the busiest tenth hold 46 to 69% of all props; an occupied square holds 2 (median) to 10 (90th percentile) indoors, 5 outdoors. Physics props per 512 x 512: indoors median 1, 90th percentile 12, most 25; outdoors median 0 to 1, 90th percentile 4 to 5. Doorways stay clear: 1 floor item within 48 of the middle of 69 doors.

- **Why:** Floor rasterised at 32 (brush tops and displacements with 64 of headroom, near AI nodes) in 18 of the 22 maps (21 November). Within 192 of a room's key furniture: kitchens 10 props (9 physics), living rooms 12 (7), hideouts 8 (5), offices 8 (5), Combine rooms 2 (0).
- **How:** Dress a module by spots, not evenly: choose 1 to 3 spots on its edges (a corner, a column foot, beside a door, never in it) and give each 6 to 12 props; leave walking lines 96 wide (valve-clutter). Keep physics at 12 or fewer per 512 square indoors and 5 outdoors (networked-budget).
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint counts props per 256 square and physics per 512 square, and warns over 12/25 indoors or an indoor room with nothing in it
- **Tags:** props, valve, performance

- **See:** `valve_c17/01_kleiner_lab_cut.png`

### Furniture against the walls, chairs pulled out (`valve-furniture-on-walls`)

Put furniture against the walls, square, facing into the room; let chairs stand free. Within 16 of a wall: shelves 76% (60% square, back to the wall), radiators 76% (all within 32), couches in flats 100%, payphones 88%, tables and desks 62%, TVs 60%, boxes 60%, trash 51%. Chairs only 22%: 33 to 58 from their table (median 42), turned to face it within 45 in 18 of 20, one to three a table. Loose barrels 32%, crates 38%, cans 12%.

- **Why:** Wall gaps (prop box to the nearest axis-aligned brush at its height) in 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November).
- **How:** Furniture placer: shelves, couches, beds, desks, stoves, sinks, bathtubs and radiators 0 to 4 from a wall with their back to it; chairs 40 out from the table, facing it plus -30..30, one in ten knocked over in a hideout or raided room.
- **Checked by:** proposed: map_lint notes shelves, couches, radiators, desks and sinks more than 16 from any wall
- **Tags:** props, valve, interiors

- **See:** `valve_c17/01_kleiner_lab_cut.png`, `valve_c17/11_combine_office_cut.png`, `valve_c17/16_flat_room_eye.png`

### Tell what happened with a few props (`valve-story-props`)

Let a small kit of props, decals and at most one sound tell the story, no text. Valve's recurring kits: a bunk room (one to three mattresses on the floor, a radio on a crate, a pallet, supplies, a rug overlay and graffiti); the dead with his weapon (a corpse, the gun 20 to 70 away, ammo, blood decals); a radio left playing beside its dead owner (the sound at the radio); a raided flat (fridge on its back with its doors off, drawers and couches tipped, boards and cinderblocks); a kitchen still in use (stove, two pots on it, cupboard, sink with bottles, the drips heard); travellers (a push cart, suitcases, one on its side, a box, by the benches). Two more from the uprising: a burned flat (burn decals on the walls, destroyedceiling models under the ceiling, a tiny fire loop, plaster dented by small displacements); a refuge being raided (couches, a fridge, bottles, a rug overlay, a crying loop, the residents and the soldiers).

- **Why:** Places in Valve's maps (21 November): c01a 2400 6240 -155, c12 476 10948 507, c10 11152 39 -375 (bunk rooms); c03 -801 -328 -905 and c12 687 6445 425 (dead with weapon); c05 7161 1635 -387 and c06 4000 5812 -151 (radio, coast.radio_dead); d1_trainstation_03 -4254 -4712 274 and -4320 -4634 440 (raided flats); -4658 -4691 276 (kitchen); d1_trainstation_01 -3958 -1711 2 (luggage). Fits the owner's 'keep text minimal' rule. d3_c17_06b (21 November): burn01a 30, burn02a 16, scorch1 7, destroyedceiling01a-d 23, streetwar.fire_tiny loops, 107 small displacements; the refuge at 3176 877 169: couches 3, fridge 2, bottles 5, decalrug002a 2, d1_trainstation.crying, four citizens and three soldiers.
- **How:** Story kits in map_mods (4 to 8 props, 1 to 3 decals, an optional ambient_generic at the prop): bunk, dead_with_gun, radio_left_on, raided_flat, kitchen, luggage. Corpses as prop_ragdoll spawnflags 4 (55 of Valve's 66).
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** props, valve, story

- **See:** `valve_c17/23_shop_and_office_cut.png`, `valve_c17/35_rubble_corner_shop_iso.png`

### A loop sits on the thing that makes it (`valve-sound-at-the-prop`)

Put a looping ambient_generic on the prop that makes the sound, a few units off it: sink drips 6 to 10 from each sink, a dead radio's static 24 to 28 from the radio, a diesel generator's hum at its pipes, printers and servers at their machines, fire loops at the burning pile. Triggered one-shots (spawnflags 48, start silent and not looped) are the commonest ambient_generic (129); always-on loops (0) are the few placed at props.

- **Why:** ambient_generic within 96 of a prop in 22 maps: d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12 and 13 Route Kanal maps (21 November): d1_trainstation_03 sinkdrips (3, at sinkkitchen01a and furnituresink001a), d1_canals_05 and 06 coast.radio_dead at citizenradio, d1_canals_11 diesel_generator, d1_trainstation_05 k_lab.printer and tech1 at servers, d3_c17_13 fire at debris piles.
- **How:** m.sound(message, u, v, z) at the prop for radios, sinks, generators, TVs and fires; radius 500 to 1250, as Valve.
- **Checked by:** review
- **Tags:** props, valve, sound

### Lightmaps fine near people, coarse far away (`valve-lightmap-by-distance`)

Never one lightmapscale for everything. 16 on what players walk past; 8 on small detailed pieces at eye level (stairs, rails, pipes, trims, a floor under a lamp); 32 to 64 on faces more than about 1000 from anywhere players walk (far walls, roof tops, backdrops, the upper part of a tall canal wall); 64 on terrain displacements, 128 on far terrain.

- **Why:** Faces within 256 of an AI node: 16 on 86% in the city maps (d1_trainstation_01 to 05, d3_c17_13, d1_town_03, d3_citadel_01, d2_coast_12), 8 or 4 on 5% (canals 11%); faces over 3000 away: 64 on 54%, 16 on only 34%. Displacements: 64 on 70 to 78% in d1_canals_01 to 13, 128 on 16% of the far ones; only near ones keep 16. 8 goes on stonefloor006a, stonewall050b/c, plasterwall, metalstair004a, metalpipe009a/b, metalrail013b, metaltrack001a. Our builder writes 16 on every face and 32 on displacements. The uprising coarsens less: lightmapscale 16 on 83% of faces, still 68% of faces more than 1024 from any node (64 on 16%, 32 on 13% there).
- **How:** map_build: a lightmapscale per box (default 16); 8 from the stair, rail, pipe and trim helpers; 64 for displacements and for pieces a module marks as backdrop or out of reach. Dark maps use more 64: Ravenholm puts lightmapscale 64 on 26% of faces in d1_town_01 and 36% in 01a (roofs, trusses, upper plaster, some floors), against 12 to 13% in the city; the caves keep 16 on every displacement.
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py lightmap (proposed): a face over 64k square units at 16 with no viewpoint or spawn within 1024; a displacement at 16 or 32.
- **Tags:** valve, lighting, materials, performance, check

### Texture scale follows the size of the surface (`valve-texture-scale`)

0.25 for what players walk past (brick, plaster, wood, small concrete). Big poured concrete on the canals at 0.5 (concretewall074a, 022a, 004c, 010c); rock and cliff faces at 1 to 2; terrain blends at 0.5 to 1; water at 0.5 to 1; walls far from play may go to 1.

- **Why:** 0.25 is on 84% of faces in the city maps and 63% in the canals, but the canals lay big concrete at 0.5 (by area: concretewall074a 53%, 022a 71%, 004c 64%, 010c 89%, 074c 90%), rockwall006a at 2 on 85%, blenddirtgrass008b at 0.5 or 1, water_canals_water2 at 0.5 or 1. Faces more than 1024 from any AI node carry scale 1 or 2 on 43% of their area (city maps), against 0.25 on 51 to 64% of the area near nodes. A 128-unit tile repeated over a 1000-unit canal wall reads as wallpaper. The uprising (d3_c17_01 to 12b, 112,670 faces) keeps 0.25 on 91% of faces and 87% of area, ruins included.
- **How:** Mod.box(tex=...) already takes a scale (map_build): pass 0.5 for canal wall panels over 256 tall, 1 or 2 for rock, cliff and terrain displacements.
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py scale (proposed): nature/rock*, cliff* or blend* faces at 0.25; a wall face longer than 1024 at 0.25.
- **Tags:** valve, materials, check

### Sit the wall texture on the floor (`valve-wall-foot-seam`)

Shift a wall texture up or down so a whole texture starts at the wall foot (at scale 0.25: v at the floor a multiple of 512 texels, a floor z on a multiple of 128), so the dirty band or dado in the texture lands at the floor and not halfway up. Leave the horizontal seams and corners as they fall.

- **Why:** 35681 walls in the city maps: the foot sits on a 512-texel seam on 36%, against 24% with no shift; the top the same (35% against 24%). Horizontal seams are left alone (30% against 27%) and corners are not wrapped: of 24840 same-material corners only 7.5% continue the texture, no more than chance. 80% of faces carry some shift, most of it from moving brushes with the texture locked.
- **How:** In the face writer of map_build, set the v shift of wall faces to -(floor z / scale) mod 512 when a box stands on a known floor; leave u at 0.
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py seam (proposed): wall faces of plasterwall, concretewall or stonewall050 whose foot is not on a 128-texel seam.
- **Tags:** valve, materials, facades

### Each lamp model has its own light recipe (`valve-fixture-lights`)

Light a fixture as Valve does that model: a light_spot a few units under the model, pitch -90, cone 60 to 80, inner 30 to 45, does the lighting; a small fixture adds a faint point light (brightness 2 to 10) for the glow. Copy the per-model table in the notes (dome light, bulb, prison lamp, cage light, fluorescent, Combine light, spotlight lamp, bell lamp).

- **Why:** Lights matched to the nearest model within 48 (946 lights, 22 maps): light_domelight02_on = light 254 216 146 at 8 plus light_spot 254 216 146 at 200, cone 60, inner 30, pitch -90 (17 pairs); lightbulb01a = light at 8 plus spot at 300, cone 75; prison_lamp001b = light at 2 plus spot at 400 to 550, cone 45 to 60 (12 pairs); light_cagelight02_on = spot 241 214 173 at 1850, cone 80, inner 45, exponent 5, 15 under the model (106 in the canals); lights_florescent01a = spot 159 237 215 at 1200, cone 80, inner 30, exponent 2; combine_light002a = spot 147 226 240 at 2000, cone 35, inner 12; light_spotlight01_lamp = spot 187 234 201 at 1500, cone 35, inner 20, pitch -30, exponent 0.5. Spots point straight down on 37% (city) and 52% (canals). Our m.lamp gives the dome light one point light, and the canal lamps are point lights at 60.
- **How:** A FIXTURE table in map_modules keyed by model; m.lamp and the canal lamps place the table light_spot under the model and turn the point light down to the table value.
- **Checked by:** map_lint.py offlamp (extend, proposed): a lit model from the table with no light_spot under it.
- **Tags:** valve, lighting

- **See:** `valve_c17/20_warehouse_hall_eye.png`
